Over the next year she threw herself into work projects and social events, but friends noticed that her usually energetic brunch gatherings and art outings became less frequent, and she started cancelling road trips and youth art program commitments at the last minute. As the months went on, her performance at work became more inconsistent—she missed deadlines, avoided collaborative meetings she once enjoyed, and quietly turned down opportunities to present her process‑improvement ideas, while at home she stopped cooking elaborate meals and let dishes and coffee cups pile up. When a close friend confronted her after she skipped a long‑planned weekend trip without explanation and admitted she was struggling to keep up with day‑to‑day life, Stacey agreed to see a mental health professional through her company’s employee assistance program, worried that she could no longer maintain the creative, outgoing lifestyle that had always defined her.\n- Chief Complaint: “My sleep is completely off—I either lie awake most of the night or crash at odd hours and still wake up exhausted.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): I keep thinking my ex and my parents have some kind of informal network at my company and are quietly feeding people information about me, like who I talk to and when I leave my desk, even though I know it sounds paranoid and no one can actually prove it’s happening.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): A few times a week, especially when I’m alone in my apartment at night, I hear this male voice that isn’t there telling me I’m a burden or that I’m being watched, and I’ll get up and check every room even though I know logically no one is in the house.\n- S022 (Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior): There are days at work when I just stand in the hallway or by the copier, frozen and staring at nothing while people walk around me, and I can’t seem to move or decide what I’m supposed to be doing until someone snaps me out of it.\n- reciting facts instead of actually feeling anything.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to organize brunches, plan art weekends, and volunteer with the youth program, but now even the thought of replying to a group text or pulling out my paints feels pointless and exhausting, so I just ignore it all and stay home.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): Most days I have to force down a protein bar or some toast because I just don’t feel hungry, and I’ve noticed my clothes getting looser even though I’m not trying to lose weight.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Lately I’ll lie in bed for hours with my mind racing, finally doze off around 3 or 4 a.m., then either jolt awake at 6 feeling wired and panicky or sleep through my alarms until late morning and still feel like I’ve been hit by a truck.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): When I’m really down, even simple things like brushing my teeth or answering an email feel like I’m moving through molasses, and my coworkers have started asking if I’m okay because I walk so slowly and take forever to respond in meetings.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I constantly replay little mistakes—like missing a deadline or snapping at my mom—and twist them into proof that I’m a horrible person who ruins everything, to the point where I’ll think maybe everyone would be better off if I just disappeared.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_depressive_episode_occur_concurrently: On my worst days, it’s like everything piles on at once—I’m dragging myself out of bed feeling empty and worthless while also hearing that man’s voice at night and being convinced my ex and my parents are secretly turning people at work against me.\n- depressive_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness_(Criterion_C): Even when the paranoia or the voice backs off a bit, the heavy, hopeless feeling and lack of motivation never really go away—I can’t remember the last time I actually felt like myself or enjoyed things for more than a day or two.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode_(Criterion_B): There was a stretch last fall where my mood felt almost neutral for a couple of weeks, but I still kept hearing the male voice at night and was sure my family and ex were watching me through people at my job, even though I wasn’t especially sad or drained during that time.\n- functional_impairment: I’ve gone from being the go‑to person for big projects and social plans to someone who barely meets minimum expectations at work, ignores texts from close friends, and lets basic chores like dishes and laundry pile up until I’m overwhelmed.\n- duration: All of this—the low mood, pulling back from people, and feeling like I’m being watched—has been building and sticking around since shortly after the breakup and fights with my parents a little over two years ago.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: The voice and the feeling that my ex and my parents are somehow monitoring me through coworkers started several months after the breakup and have been coming and going for well over a year now.\n- depressive_episode_duration: The deep, ongoing depression—losing interest in everything, feeling worthless, and struggling to function—has been there most days since the first few months after the breakup, so a little over two years.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can replying to a group text or pulling out my paints feels pointless and exhausting, so I just ignore it all and stay home.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): Most days I have to force down a protein bar or some toast because I just don’t feel hungry, and I’ve noticed my clothes getting looser even though I’m not trying to lose weight.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Lately I’ll lie in bed for hours with my mind racing, finally doze off around 3 or 4 a.m., then either jolt awake at 6 feeling wired and panicky or sleep through my alarms until late morning and still feel like I’ve been hit by a truck.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): When I’m really down, even simple things like brushing my teeth or answering an email feel like I’m moving through molasses, and my coworkers have started asking if I’m okay because I walk so slowly and take forever to respond in meetings.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I constantly replay little mistakes—like missing a deadline or snapping at my mom—and twist them into proof that I’m a horrible person who ruins everything, to the point where I’ll think maybe everyone would be better off if I just disappeared.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_depressive_episode_occur_concurrently: On my worst days, it’s like everything piles on at once—I’m dragging myself out of bed feeling empty and worthless while also hearing that man’s voice at night and being convinced my ex and my parents are secretly turning people at work against me.\n- depressive_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness_(Criterion_C): Even when the paranoia or the voice backs off a bit, the heavy, hopeless feeling and lack of motivation never really go away—I can’t remember the last time I actually felt like myself or enjoyed things for more than a day or two.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode_(Criterion_B): There was a stretch last fall where my mood felt almost neutral for a couple of weeks, but I still kept hearing the male voice at night and was sure my family and ex were watching me through people at my job, even though I wasn’t especially sad or drained during that time.\n- functional_impairment: I’ve gone from being the go‑to person for big projects and social plans to someone who barely meets minimum expectations at work, ignores texts from close friends, and lets Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick). Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.  Subtypes: Recurrent Thoughts of Death, Suicidal Ideation, Suicidal Impulse, Specific Plan for Suicide, Suicide Attempt\n[S083] Hopelessness: A feeling of despair or having no hope for the future. Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions). Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.
